## Scope, evidence rules and limitations

### Requested scope

This document covers every identifiable test, diagnostic, failure/retry, administrative transition, artifact manipulation and setup interruption in the retained conversation, together with all five available uploaded text records and all ten screenshots of the web-control test. It also carries the full set of findings from the subsequent code review and the fixes/regressions proposed for each.

It does not invent missing shell output, reconstruct an unavailable local GPU-agent final report, or treat an unexecuted instruction as an executed test. Earlier messages omitted from the retained conversation cannot be reproduced verbatim; relevant uploaded baseline and adversarial-review records were retrieved to recover their available evidence. The exact initial upstream-repair output, standalone Codex Security report, precise per-test inventory of later suites and all historical process measurements were not supplied. These absences remain explicit.

No fresh live test was run against Lane's machine while compiling this document. Source review used connected GitHub reads; file processing only assembled local report artifacts. Historical `C:\Users\<user>` and `/mnt/c/Users/<user>` paths are operator-machine paths, not paths available to the report-generation environment.

### Evidence categories

| Category | Meaning | What it cannot establish on its own |
|---|---|---|
| **OBSERVED** | Output or screenshot supplied by the operator | Behavior outside the shown run, hidden process state, broad guarantees |
| **REPORTED** | Agent/operator says a command/suite passed | Independent rerun, complete raw test inventory or uncovered scenarios |
| **CODE-CONFIRMED** | Inspected source contains the described contract/order/default | That every predicted timing-dependent failure has occurred in practice |
| **RISK / NEEDS REPRODUCTION** | Plausible mechanism/interleaving supported by source | Confirmed exploit, duplicate side effect or user-facing hang |
| **VALIDATION GAP** | Required assertion was never completed in this session | That the implementation necessarily fails that assertion |
| **GUIDANCE ONLY** | A command/plan was proposed without an executed result | Installation, state mutation or successful verification |

Expected security refusals are positive test results: unauthorized/forbidden requests, expired approvals, digest-mismatched skills, audit mismatch and corrupted-startup refusal. Distinguish them from failures of the test harness or user experience around them.

### Revision and reproducibility limits

The final source review is pinned to `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94`. PR metadata and the agent report agree on this head. The displayed manual commands ran in `lumen-m5-windows`, but no `git rev-parse HEAD`, dirty-tree inventory or binary hash was captured alongside every manual run. It is therefore the intended/reported manual-test baseline, not cryptographic proof that every local binary corresponded to the same clean commit. Source findings apply to the pinned code; future fixes must recheck any later head.

Time values in stored audit events are historical data, not reliable per-operation elapsed-time measurements: F06 explains the frozen timestamp mechanism. Human timing and screenshots are separate evidence. The report converts recorded epochs with the stated timezone but does not retroactively correct or rewrite source logs.

### Privacy and safety of this report

The disposable bearer token was visible in the working conversation, but is omitted from this distributable Markdown and issue bodies. Sanitized copies preserve the supplied technical content while replacing the token, personal path segments, and machine-local evidence links. The evidence manifest records both original-file SHA-256 and packaged-copy SHA-256 so redaction is transparent. The only SSH key printed in the transcript was a public key; no private key bytes were provided. Source bodies and screenshots should still be treated as project-internal material.

## Executive acceptance decision

### What passed within the observed scope

| Area | Supported result | Important qualification |
|---|---|---|
| SQLite migration | `Migrated` returned for functional and clean web fixtures | Not a same-DB restart test |
| Sandbox detection | `linux-bubblewrap`, `KernelEnforced`, stated guarantees | Detection report, not live adversarial sandbox escape test |
| Local authentication | Invalid token rejected; valid token reached real routes | `/health` itself was nonexistent |
| Workspace restriction | Browser using wrong ID denied; corrected ID worked | Green header did not actually verify connectivity |
| Service identities | `functional-test` and `web-test` created with explicit owners/grants | No forbidden zero-grant tool action attempted |
| Scheduled administration | Creation and enablement changes required approvals | Positive grant paths only, aside from expiry refusal |
| One-shot scheduling | Expected occurrence attributed to service and recorded completed | Occurrence terminal row/output/exactly-once across crashes not checked |
| Workflow capture/publish | Draft created; publish approval led to reviewed/enabled skill | Empty-action provenance, not meaningful procedure reuse |
| Reviewed skill selection | Valid digest included in loaded_skills | Actual task improvement not measured |
| Skill tamper rejection | Modified artifact excluded from loaded_skills | Exclusion was silent; no oversize memory test |
| Audit chain | Healthy verification, tamper detection, startup refusal all observed | Not proof against privileged full-chain replacement |
| Web pause/resume | Pending state remained persisted state; granted transitions rev1→rev2→rev3 | Rejection, concurrency, stale-tab and restart cases untested |
| GPU sample | Qwen reported 100% GPU under Ollama | Not universal/persistent GPU-only policy or request attribution |

### What must not be claimed

Do not call this session complete functional acceptance, production readiness, a full local-agent tool test, a proven meaningful learned workflow, exactly-once external execution, universal GPU enforcement, a clean-shutdown proof, or full Linux desktop verification. The original broad pass declarations are superseded by this document, not silently erased.

### Priority policy

**P1:** integrity or essential acceptance gate; resolve/reproduce with evidence before recommending the affected M5 path accepted. **P2:** important behavior/usability/robustness work; address before broader user testing or explicitly defer with an owner. **P3:** bounded documentation/presentation/environment follow-up. These are triage recommendations, not CVSS ratings, exploitability claims or formal repository milestones.

There are **72 chronological cases** and **24 report observations**, mapped to **22 canonical GitHub issues**. Some observations share a root cause and implementation scope; the combined Rust/JavaScript setup observation maps to two separate issues. Every observation has a verified issue link. Report observation IDs F01–F24 and GitHub QA finding IDs F01–F22 are separate numbering systems; use the explicit crosswalk, not a numeric guess. Historical repairs already included in PR #6 are retained as repaired history rather than refiled as new defects.

## Automated verification and repair history

### Baseline failure details

The raw Windows log is included in full in Appendix A, E01. It contains all 53 test-result lines, every printed failure detail, both compiler warnings and the final `5 passed; 48 failed` summary. The recurring TOML error was `too few unicode value digits, expected unicode hexadecimal value`, caused when a raw Windows path in a TOML basic string reached a backslash-U sequence. Several failures used nonexistent Unix executable fixtures (`echo executable` / `cat executable`). These are setup/common-root failures; do not count them as independent proofs that every named security guarantee was violated.

### Initial repair state

The first agent report described 21 changed files and two commits:

| Commit | Reported purpose |
|---|---|
| `713481d497fc6134c76ded040d0e756fae1994b0` | `fix(runtime): make Windows paths and processes portable` |
| `c4b5bc7a5c59f0cd2fccb6030749d885fa6f555f` | `fix(automation): preserve durable execution boundaries` |

It reported 75 CLI / 264 workspace Windows tests, 12 frontend units, 14 browser tests and clean static checks. WSL CLI reported 76. Full Linux desktop prerequisites were missing. One wider parallel test run hit an existing two-second deadline and then passed in isolation. The precise test identity and raw failure log were not provided; retain this as an unresolved historical timing observation, not an excuse to increase all timeouts.

### Follow-up repaired findings (historical, not new issues)

| Repair ID | Finding in earlier revision | Reported repair/regression | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| R01 | Windows path interpolation generated invalid TOML | Serializer-backed paths and quote/backslash/Unicode cases | Included in PR #6 |
| R02 | Unix executable/signal assumptions on Windows | Platform fixtures/gates without widening executor authority | Included; platform coverage still explicit |
| R03 | Host separators in package/artifact paths | Portable `/`-relative persisted paths | Included |
| R04 | Existing-file replacement behavior and pathname handoff | Initially ReplaceFileW; then handle-relative rename from open temp/parent handles | Final implementation supersedes initial description |
| R05 | Windows warning-denied build branches | Appropriate platform handling | Reported clean clippy |
| R06 | Scheduled job identity/revision weak invariants | Immutable identity, consecutive checked revisions | Included |
| R07 | Transactional claim/revision eligibility | BEGIN IMMEDIATE and two-connection barriers | Included; earlier lock conflict was fail-closed |
| R08 | Capture gathered unrelated / truncated audit records | Indexed exact run/workspace query; 1,001 older records and later unrelated run | Included |
| R09 | Egress audit events lacked source run correlation | run_id required and captured egress regression | Included |
| R10 | Cross-workspace capture inserted a draft before rejection | Bind workspace in initial lookup; no-draft regression | Included |
| R11 | Optimistic unapproved pause/resume display | Keep persisted state pending approval | Manually observed positive flow |
| R12 | Saturating revision overflow hid exhaustion | Checked increment and i64::MAX regression | Included |

The agent report describes a three-finding standalone scan (two medium, one low) and six total follow-up repair rows, because not all additional review/concurrency observations came from that one scan. Do not misreport those as six scan findings. Its local report path was a reference, not an attached file. Full report E02 is reproduced below.

F23 requires a named-test accounting; it must not be treated as proof of weakened coverage or proof of equivalence. E02 states no assertion weakened, no timeout enlarged and no newly ignored test.

<a id="t01"></a>
### T01 — Initial branch tracking failure

**Evidence:** Observed terminal output

**Action:** In PowerShell, fetched origin, switched to feat/milestone-5-durable-automation and attempted git pull.

**Observed / reported result:** Git said the branch was already selected but had no tracking information. It suggested an explicit remote/branch or setting upstream.

**Assessment:** Environment/setup obstacle, not a Lumen runtime failure.

**Limits / correction / follow-up:** The exact upstream-repair command/result is not retained in the available record. Do not infer a branch merge or assume every later local working tree was clean.

**Historical command or representative exact request:**
```bash
git fetch origin
git switch feat/milestone-5-durable-automation
git pull
```

**Key output:**
```text
Already on 'feat/milestone-5-durable-automation'
There is no tracking information for the current branch.
Please specify which branch you want to merge with.
```

<a id="t03"></a>
### T03 — Native Windows CLI test baseline

**Evidence:** Observed complete uploaded log E01

**Action:** Ran cargo test -p lumen-cli in C:\Users\<user>\lumen.

**Observed / reported result:** Compiled in 1m 07s; 53 library tests ran. Five passed, 48 failed, zero ignored, measured or filtered. Test execution reported 0.04s. Two warnings named resource_limits and process_id. Most failures arose before behavior under test: unescaped Windows TOML paths; others tried Unix echo/cat executables.

**Assessment:** Fail: portable test setup and platform handling needed repair.

**Limits / correction / follow-up:** 48 failed test names do not establish 48 independent exploitable product defects. The complete names, stack locations, TOML messages, compiler output and counts are in Appendix A/E01.

**Implementation / reference:** [S01](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/pull/6)

**Assessment:** Installation succeeded; duplicate serve was unnecessary.

```bash
ollama serve
ollama serve
```

```text
Error: listen tcp 127.0.0.1:11434: bind: address already in use
```

**Evidence:** User report and subsequent completion acknowledgement

**Observed / reported result:** No memory counters before/after, process inventory, .wslconfig or actual shutdown command output pasted.

**Assessment:** User reports environment shut down; memory issue unresolved as diagnosis.

**Limits / correction / follow-up:** Do not label Lumen memory leak or choose RAM cap from an assumed host total. WSL VM termination is not a graceful app-shutdown test. Docker autorestart was suggested as a possibility, not observed.

**Historical command or representative exact request:**

```bash
wsl --shutdown
```

## Observed API, artifact and timing evidence

### API request shapes actually exercised

These are historical shapes, with symbolic IDs and redacted credentials. They are not new routes or a proposed replacement API.

| Operation | Method/path | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| List jobs | `GET /api/v1/workspaces/{workspace}/automation/jobs` | Empty list, approved job and consumed next_due_at |
| Upsert service | `POST .../automation/service-identities` | functional-test and web-test |
| Request job creation/update | `POST .../automation/jobs/{job_id}` | Approval requested; pause/resume UI uses this route |
| List approvals | `GET .../approvals` | Fresh and expired pending entries |
| Decide approval | `POST .../approvals/{approval_id}/decision` | `grant`, expiry failure; Reject not executed in manual pass |
| List audit | `GET .../audit?after=0&limit=100` | Full 28-event evidence; 200 worked, 250 rejected |
| Capture workflow | `POST .../skills/capture-drafts` | Completed scheduled-run draft |
| Publish draft | `POST .../skills/capture-drafts/{draft_id}/publish` | Approval request |
| List skills | `GET .../skills` | Reviewed/enabled metadata |
| Create agent run | `POST .../runs` | Public prompts before/after skill mutation |
| Read final run events | `GET .../runs/{run_id}/events` | Exists in source; not actually retrieved during manual verification |
| Health path | `GET /health` | Does not exist; auth first returned 401, then routing 404 |

The valid listing bounds are default 100, 1≤limit≤200, after≥0. Backend bound enforcement was correct. Future test helpers must detect error envelopes instead of treating missing `.events` as an empty collection.

The web job changed service to `web-test`, prompt to `Web control test`, max_model_turns to 1 and run_at to 1789261284948, while keeping max_actions=1, public, enabled and idempotent. UI updates changed enabled while carrying the other shown fields.

### Approval and schedule timing

| Purpose | Created | Expires | Scheduled date | Key conclusion |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| First request | 1789255346174 | 1789255646174 | 1789255646163 | Five-minute TTL; first grant attempted too late |
| Replacement request | 1789256058528 | 1789256358528 | 1789256658516 | Still five-minute TTL despite ten-minute schedule |
| Successful job persisted | 1789256085808 | Not an approval field | 1789256658516 | Creation happens after grant/dispatch |
| Scheduled lifecycle start | 1789256661663 | Not applicable | 1789256658516 | Recorded start 3,147 ms after scheduled_for |

The source scheduler polls at 60-second intervals. A single observed 3.147-second offset does not establish maximum scheduling latency, jitter, or correct behavior under load. A five-second post-due sleep is insufficient as a general acceptance deadline. Completion timestamps reused creation time, so generation duration is not derivable from that pair.

### Audit schema observed before mutation


```sql
CREATE TABLE audit_events (
    sequence INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
    event_id TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE,
    timestamp INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (timestamp >= 0),
    event_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    outcome TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (outcome IN ('success', 'failure', 'denied', 'pending', 'unknown')),
    workspace_id TEXT REFERENCES workspaces(id) ON DELETE RESTRICT,
    payload_json TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (json_valid(payload_json)),
    previous_hash TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (
        length(previous_hash) = 64 AND previous_hash NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*'
    ),
    event_hash TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE CHECK (
        length(event_hash) = 64 AND event_hash NOT GLOB '*[^0-9a-f]*'
    )
) STRICT;
CREATE TABLE audit_checkpoints (
    sequence INTEGER PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES audit_events(sequence) ON DELETE RESTRICT,
    event_hash TEXT NOT NULL,
    verified_at INTEGER NOT NULL
) STRICT;
```

The intentional SQL changed only `payload_json` at sequence 28, leaving stored hashes untouched. The healthy verifier previously passed; both `audit verify` and `serve` then rejected the modified content. This is an observed negative-security pass. No backup was made before mutation, no numeric CLI exit code was recorded, and no attempt to rewrite/reanchor the entire chain was tested.

<a id="f01"></a>
### F01 — Complete the live OpenAI-compatible tool-call round trip and verify real results

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#7](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/7) (QA F01)

#### Summary

The acceptance pass never verified the final Lumen answer or a real model-selected tool action. The adapter can parse a tool call but its ChatRequest only supplies model, messages and stream; no tool definitions are advertised.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F01 |
| Priority | P1 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | models / integration / acceptance |
| Evidence | CODE-CONFIRMED request-contract omission; MANUAL VALIDATION GAP, not a reproduced failed tool task |
| Historical test cases | T16, T19, T38, T39, T42, T43, T44 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

Text-only runs were accepted and audit lifecycle completion was recorded. A separately invoked Ollama API refused the harmless OPENAI_COMPAT_OK marker. ollama ps showed GPU residency, which cannot identify a particular Lumen run. No filesystem action, model follow-up using its result, or actual final response was captured.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

Inspect OpenAiCompatibleClient::send, ChatRequest, request-message serialization, output_from_parts/output_from_accumulated, and RunOrchestrator message accumulation. Parsing an unsolicited tool call is not a complete provider protocol. Complete protocol handling must be verified rather than assuming all providers accept the internal message representation.

#### Impact

An agent runtime may appear healthy while accomplishing only chat or accepting model prose that claims tools ran. Missing schemas can prevent ordinary tool selection. Poor result correlation can conceal integration failure or lose exact call identity.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Define provider-neutral tool descriptors and call/result IDs, deriving the offered catalog from runtime-owned registered tools and current permissions, not from a model-supplied authority list.
2. Serialize supported function schemas into the OpenAI-compatible request. Preserve assistant tool-call envelopes and bind each returned tool result to the corresponding call ID using the provider contract; retain reasoning fields only where the adapter contract requires them.
3. Validate tool name, arguments and supported call multiplicity. Do not concatenate unrelated streamed calls. If only one call per turn is supported, reject unsupported multiplicity clearly rather than silently discarding it.
4. Continue routing every action through normalization, capability/policy checks, approval and existing dispatch/audit. Never convert freeform prose into privileged execution or add an unrestricted shell to make a test pass.
5. Add wire-level deterministic integration fixtures and a separately marked live Ollama acceptance test. Use a random nonce stored only in a disposable file so the model cannot answer correctly without the actual read.
6. Capture the run ID, provider/model identity, final answer and corresponding action/side-effect evidence. Record missing usage as unknown; do not infer attribution from model residency alone.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Text-only response is returned to UI/API and asserted, including legitimate nonmatching/refusal content as a failed task assertion rather than transport failure.
- [ ] Read nonce.txt through the real model and assert exact nonce in final answer plus one authorized filesystem read.
- [ ] Request a small output file, confirm absent before approval, grant, then verify exact bytes and model acknowledgement; repeat with reject and assert no write.
- [ ] Cover unknown tool, malformed arguments, mismatched IDs, multiple/interleaved streamed calls, empty content, provider failure and cancellation without bypass.
- [ ] Re-run same request under a zero-grant service and assert no prohibited effect.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Outbound request has correct tool schema and follow-up correspondence for the supported provider.
- [ ] Live nonce read and approval-gated write pass with real final output and side-effect evidence.
- [ ] Denied/rejected requests cause zero unauthorized writes.
- [ ] No claim that a run succeeded is based solely on ollama ps or lifecycle events.

#### Non-goals and cautions

Fix the current single-provider integration only. Do not implement the full multi-model roadmap from #4 or the compartment architecture from #5. A suspected end-to-end failure must first be reproduced; label the missing schema separately from untested protocol concerns.

<a id="f02"></a>
### F02 — Preserve aggregate wall-time and captured-output quotas for scheduled runs

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#9](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/9) (QA F03)

#### Summary

Scheduled jobs construct a new RunBudget with only per-job turn/action limits and omit the aggregate quotas configured for interactive runs.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F02 |
| Priority | P1 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | automation / resource limits |
| Evidence | CODE-CONFIRMED; not manually stress-tested |
| Historical test cases | T31, T33, T38 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

The manual job used at most two model turns and one action; it never attempted a slow or output-heavy workload. Normal composition calls RunBudget::new(...).with_quotas(...), while run_due_scheduled_job uses only RunBudget::new(job.max_model_turns(), job.max_actions()).

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

RunBudget::new defaults max_wall_time to None and max_captured_result_bytes to usize::MAX. Rebuilding the budget from two job fields drops the configured whole-run limits, even if individual requests/executors remain bounded.

#### Impact

Scheduled execution has weaker resource constraints than the same interactive workload. Per-operation timeouts do not replace an aggregate execution budget.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Introduce one explicit budget-derivation path shared by interactive and scheduled runs. Preserve runtime wall-time/output caps while applying per-job turn/action settings.
2. Define whether job turn/action maxima may exceed runtime policy. Prefer policy ceilings plus per-job tightening; reject unsupported widening instead of silently converting it to unlimited.
3. Validate zero/overflow cases and conversions at configuration boundaries. Do not use sentinel unlimited values accidentally through a constructor.
4. Use monotonic elapsed time for enforcement; specify whether awaiting approval counts toward the whole-run deadline and apply that policy consistently.
5. Emit a clear budget-exhausted outcome and persist the corresponding scheduled occurrence state. Retain lower executor/provider limits.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Equivalent interactive and scheduled slow workloads hit the configured whole-run deadline.
- [ ] Many individually bounded results cumulatively exceed the captured-output cap and terminate predictably.
- [ ] A job may tighten but cannot unintentionally erase/widen policy ceilings.
- [ ] Budget exhaustion near approval, cancellation and provider timeout is attributed to the correct cause.
- [ ] Use controlled clocks/barriers rather than arbitrary long sleeps.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] No scheduled path falls back to constructor defaults for configured aggregate quotas.
- [ ] Turn, action, wall-time and cumulative-output limits have explicit tested derivation.
- [ ] Job/run terminal states and audit/error output agree after exhaustion.

#### Non-goals and cautions

Do not claim all scheduled execution was unlimited; individual limits already exist. Preserve those guards and add the missing aggregate policy.

#### Related QA work

QA F04, QA F06. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

- [S02: runtime composition, scheduler, skill loading, finalization](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/crates/lumen-cli/src/runtime.rs)
- [S03: run orchestration and RunBudget defaults](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/crates/lumen-core/src/run.rs)

<a id="f03"></a>
### F03 — Persist scheduled occurrence/run linkage before starting execution

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#10](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/10) (QA F04)

#### Summary

The scheduler starts execution inside create_stored_run before linking the durable occurrence record to that run and advancing next_due_at.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F03 |
| Priority | P1 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | automation / transactions / concurrency |
| Evidence | CODE-CONFIRMED ordering; RACE HYPOTHESIS requiring deterministic reproduction |
| Historical test cases | T35, T37, T38 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

The observed one-shot yielded lifecycle completion and next_due_at null. The test did not inspect scheduled_job_runs, so it cannot exclude a stale running occurrence after a fast completion.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

run_due_scheduled_job claims an occurrence, calls create_stored_run (which calls spawn_advance), then mark_scheduled_occurrence_running and advance_scheduled_job_next_due. Completion updates WHERE run_id = ?, which may match no occurrence if execution wins the handoff race. The repository completion method does not verify affected rows.

#### Impact

An immediately completing run may finish before its occurrence linkage exists, after which the scheduler writes running. A crash between reservation, linking, spawning and advancement also needs a recoverable protocol. This is a static interleaving analysis, not an observed duplicate or exploit.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Separate run preparation/persistence from run activation. Execution must not be eligible before a durable occurrence-to-run relationship exists.
2. Define an explicit state machine: eligible, claimed/reserved, associated/prepared, running, terminal/unknown. Decide which transitions commit atomically with next_due_at advancement; avoid a transaction spanning model/network work.
3. Bind transitions to the exact occurrence key, revision and current lease ownership. Validate affected-row counts and reject stale actors rather than silently advancing.
4. Document the commit/spawn crash window and recovery rule. A crash must not lose due work or automatically replay a non-idempotent side effect.
5. Retain immutable job identity, consecutive revisions, BEGIN IMMEDIATE eligibility checks and prior two-connection regression coverage.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Barrier test forces model completion before the old linkage point; old code should reproduce inconsistent occurrence state, repaired code should not permit activation that early.
- [ ] Assert final scheduled_job_runs row, agent_runs row, occurrence key and exactly one relevant run—not just audit lifecycle.
- [ ] Crash/inject failure before association, after association before spawn, and after spawn before scheduling advancement; restart same DB and reconcile.
- [ ] Two claimers and a stale lease owner cannot attach/complete different runs for the same occurrence.
- [ ] Non-idempotent unknown remains explicit pending reconciliation.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] No worker/model call begins before required durable linkage exists.
- [ ] An immediate success never leaves its occurrence running/claimed.
- [ ] Every injected crash window has documented tested behavior without an unqualified exactly-once guarantee.
- [ ] State transitions validate ownership and affected rows.

#### Non-goals and cautions

Do not solve by adding sleeps or increasing lease time. Do not equate a unique occurrence key with exactly-once external effects.

#### Related QA work

QA F04. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

#### Sources

- [S02: runtime composition, scheduler, skill loading, finalization](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/crates/lumen-cli/src/runtime.rs)
- [S04: scheduled occurrence repository, claims and transitions](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/crates/lumen-db/src/automation.rs)

<a id="f04"></a>
### F04 — Reconcile failed scheduled runs and stop ignoring required persistence errors

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#11](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/11) (QA F05)

#### Summary

Successful outcomes update the scheduled occurrence, but the error branch updates only the agent run; multiple state-persistence results are discarded.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F04 |
| Priority | P1 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | automation / state integrity |
| Evidence | CODE-CONFIRMED asymmetry and discarded results; failure path not manually exercised |
| Historical test cases | T38, T43, T48 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

Only successful short model runs were observed. The run/occurrence repository was not queried after model errors, cancellation, or storage failures.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

In LocalRuntimeService::advance, the Ok(outcome) branch calls complete_scheduled_occurrence_for_run; Err(error) does not perform that occurrence transition. update_run_state and occurrence updates commonly use let _ = result. complete_scheduled_occurrence_for_run currently accepts zero affected rows as success.

#### Impact

Provider outage can leave a failed run paired with a running occurrence. Durable write failure may be hidden behind an emitted terminal UI event, weakening recovery, audit interpretation and operator trust.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Centralize finalization with explicit mappings for completed, denied, rejected, exhausted, failed, cancelled and execution-unknown outcomes.
2. Persist related run and occurrence transitions consistently, preferably transactionally when appropriate. Check row counts and make retry/idempotency semantics explicit.
3. Do not report committed success if a required durable transition failed. Surface a storage/degraded-state error and preserve enough context for reconciliation.
4. Audit meaningful terminal failures with safe run/job/revision/occurrence correlation and nonsecret diagnostics. Preserve original provider failure separately from any persistence failure.
5. Keep in-memory cleanup independent from hiding failed durable transitions; define recovery behavior after process loss.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Unavailable Ollama port, HTTP failure, timeout and malformed provider JSON produce a terminal failed/appropriate occurrence, not stale running.
- [ ] Cancellation and budget exhaustion lead to consistent run and occurrence records.
- [ ] Inject DB busy/write errors during run finalization and prove error remains visible and state is recoverable.
- [ ] Zero-row transition is reported as conflict unless explicitly recognized idempotent replay.
- [ ] Repeated finalization and restart reconciliation cannot overwrite a newer state.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] All terminal paths update or explicitly quarantine/reconcile both state records.
- [ ] Required write errors and zero-row conflicts are not silently discarded.
- [ ] No normal completed UI outcome is emitted as if durable state committed when it did not.
- [ ] Regression includes a real provider-unavailable scenario.

#### Non-goals and cautions

Do not recompute audit history or fabricate successful terminal rows. Unknown external effect is distinct from clean failure.

#### Related QA work

QA F03. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

#### Sources

- [S02: runtime composition, scheduler, skill loading, finalization](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/crates/lumen-cli/src/runtime.rs)
- [S04: scheduled occurrence repository, claims and transitions](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/crates/lumen-db/src/automation.rs)

<a id="f05"></a>
### F05 — Expose scheduler poll failures and publish meaningful scheduling progress

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#11](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/11) (QA F05)

#### Summary

The periodic scheduler ignores its poll result, while the UI/API do not provide enough progress to distinguish waiting from failure.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F05 |
| Priority | P1 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | automation / observability |
| Evidence | CODE-CONFIRMED ignored loop errors; OBSERVED operator confusion about waiting |
| Historical test cases | T36, T37, T38 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

Operator waited on a shell sleep and asked whether the job was stuck. It eventually fired, but no scheduler status made the delay understandable.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

scheduled_job_loop sleeps for 60 seconds and evaluates let _ = self.run_due_scheduled_jobs_once(now()).await. A load/claim error can therefore disappear. run_due_scheduled_jobs_once returns on the first propagated job error, making later-job behavior worth explicit testing.

#### Impact

Operators cannot distinguish a not-yet-polled due job from a failing scheduler. A due+5-second test deadline is incompatible with a generally 60-second poll cadence.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Report scheduler running/stopping/degraded status, last poll, last successful poll, next expected poll and safe last-error metadata through the existing runtime diagnostic layer.
2. Handle poll errors explicitly with structured logs and stable error categories; avoid busy loops and sensitive prompt content in diagnostics.
3. Define per-job fault isolation: one invalid job should not silently starve independent eligible jobs; record each failure and continue only when safe.
4. Document cadence and expected scheduling granularity. Make a bounded configurable cadence only if justified; do not promise real-time firing.
5. Expose scheduled_for, claimed/started/completed timestamps and lateness from actual occurrence records for test inspection.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Inject due-job query failure and show a degraded state rather than silent idle.
- [ ] A faulty job followed by a healthy eligible job exercises chosen fault-isolation behavior.
- [ ] No repeated tight-loop logging or retry storm.
- [ ] Test harness polls with a deadline accounting for documented cadence and distinguishes waiting/failed/completed.
- [ ] Shutdown immediately stops new poll dispatch.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] No scheduler-loop error is discarded without a surfaced diagnostic.
- [ ] Waiting progress is inspectable and timing contract is documented.
- [ ] Failure of one due job has explicit tested effect on the remainder.

#### Non-goals and cautions

The observed job was not itself stuck. This issue improves diagnosis and error handling rather than treating the intentional sleep as a runtime failure.

#### Related QA work

QA F04, QA F06, QA F16. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

<a id="f06"></a>
### F06 — Timestamp audit events at their occurrence using an injectable clock

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#12](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/12) (QA F06)

#### Summary

A single timestamp supplied to run_until_blocked is reused across events emitted before and after awaited work.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F06 |
| Priority | P2 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | audit / time |
| Evidence | OBSERVED identical lifecycle timestamps and CODE-CONFIRMED reuse |
| Historical test cases | T38, T43, T48 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

Scheduled created/completed both 1789256661663; model-run created/completed both 1789256797739; tamper-run pair both 1789256869452. No true inference duration is recoverable from these pairs.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

RunOrchestrator::run_until_blocked accepts now: TimestampMillis and passes it to audit and approval/execution helpers throughout a loop that awaits model generation. Event wall-clock time is frozen at invocation entry.

#### Impact

Timeline latency and forensic ordering are misleading. Audit verification can prove stored bytes match hashes while the timestamps still describe the wrong real instant.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Introduce a clock abstraction owned by the runtime and injectable in tests. Sample wall-clock time when an event/action decision is actually recorded; retain monotonic clocks for elapsed budget/timeout enforcement.
2. Review uses of the same now value in approval creation/consumption and dispatch. Validate decisions against current time where required without asserting an unproven expiry bypass.
3. Define clock rollback behavior: sequence remains authoritative ordering; do not fabricate monotonic wall timestamps or erase prior events.
4. Preserve deterministic testing using a controlled clock advanced across awaited barriers rather than zero-sleep timing assumptions.
5. Record meaningful start/end metadata for provider work without dumping raw prompt or internal model reasoning into audit logs.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Advance fake clock between model invocation and response: completed timestamp reflects second instant.
- [ ] Advance clock across approval expiry/dispatch boundary and verify stale authorization remains denied.
- [ ] Cover wall-clock rollback while monotonic budget still expires correctly.
- [ ] Audit-chain validation still passes and no existing rows are rewritten.
- [ ] A live smoke test records plausible elapsed interval without a hard latency performance assertion.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Audit events no longer inherit a single stale call-entry timestamp for an entire model turn.
- [ ] Authorization-time and elapsed-time policies are explicit and tested.
- [ ] Historical logs remain immutable; only newly emitted records change.

#### Non-goals and cautions

This is not proof of fake inference or an established approval-expiry exploit. Keep those claims out of implementation reports unless separately reproduced.

- [S02: runtime composition, scheduler, skill loading, finalization](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/crates/lumen-cli/src/runtime.rs)

<a id="f07"></a>
### F07 — Expire approval queue entries and distinguish expired from changed-action conflicts

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#13](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/13) (QA F07)

#### Summary

Expired approvals remain in the pending response and the UI presents grants without checking expiry; every 409 is translated as Action changed.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F07 |
| Priority | P2 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | approvals / API / web |
| Evidence | OBSERVED stale entry and expired grant rejection; CODE-CONFIRMED UI conflation |
| Historical test cases | T32, T33, T34, T64, T67, T69 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

Grant of 151624c9... correctly returned approval expired; later list still included it alongside 6d6002a6.... A ten-minute scheduled date did not change the five-minute approval TTL.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

list_pending_approvals filters state=pending but not expiry. ApprovalItem disables by busy state only. approvals/+page.svelte catches all 409 statuses and substitutes Action changed. Review the refreshed request.

#### Impact

Operators see ungrantable work counted as pending, receive the wrong remediation, and can repeatedly retry expired requests. This is not a bypass: backend expiry rejection passed.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Define explicit expiration semantics, including whether expiry is persisted via an audited lifecycle transition or derived for reads. Do not perform unaudited mutations merely to hide entries.
2. Exclude expired entries from actionable pending counts, or display them separately as expired with decisions disabled.
3. Expose machine-readable conflict reasons such as approval_expired, already_decided and action_changed while preserving safe human messages.
4. Update the browser countdown against server time/expiry and disable controls promptly; refresh on expiry and connection changes.
5. Document independent schedule time and approval lifetime. Fresh approval must rebind the current normalized action/fingerprint; never extend an expired authorization in place.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Advance controlled time beyond TTL: pending count is correct, expired entry cannot be granted via API or UI.
- [ ] Verify UI distinguishes expiry, changed fingerprint, already decided and unavailable runtime.
- [ ] Test expiry while page remains open and during click/network latency.
- [ ] Fresh request after expiry has a new approval and correctly reviewed action.
- [ ] Reject path, repeated grant and concurrent decisions preserve one-shot consumption.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Expired approvals are visibly expired/nonactionable and not counted as fresh pending work.
- [ ] 409 handling reports the correct actionable cause.
- [ ] Backend expiry enforcement is not weakened.

#### Non-goals and cautions

Do not lengthen production TTL solely to accommodate conversational testing. The test harness should avoid unnecessary human/tool round trips.

#### Sources

<a id="f08"></a>
### F08 — Verify authenticated workspace connectivity before showing connected

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#14](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/14) (QA F08)

#### Summary

The Local runtime green status currently means three connection fields are nonempty, not that the server/auth/workspace check succeeded.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F08 |
| Priority | P2 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | web / connection |
| Evidence | OBSERVED false connected indicator and CODE-CONFIRMED configured predicate |
| Historical test cases | T59, T63, T64 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

Browser showed green Local runtime while Approvals returned workspace is not allowlisted. Replacing old W1 with clean W2 corrected the load.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

ConnectionDialog saves trimmed settings without a probe. isConfigured checks only baseUrl/workspaceId/token truthiness. Header uses that predicate as connected state.

#### Impact

Operators can believe the wrong or offline workspace is active. This wastes diagnosis time and encourages unsafe assumptions about what an action will target.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Separate syntactic configured state from authenticated connection health. Define unconfigured, checking, connected, unauthorized, workspace_denied, unreachable and disconnected/degraded states.
2. Use a documented authenticated workspace-scoped, read-only readiness/capability request. Do not infer connectivity from unauthenticated 404/401 or expose tokens in URL/logs.
3. When saving settings, invalidate prior connection health and only show success for the exact normalized base URL/workspace/token generation verified.
4. Cancel/ignore stale probe responses and show current workspace ID/name with useful errors. Do not silently switch workspace based on a server suggestion.
5. Revalidate after failures/restart; use explicit user retry or bounded health checks without intrusive background behavior.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Nonempty invalid token never turns status green.
- [ ] Wrong workspace produces workspace_denied while correct token/base are preserved.
- [ ] Stopped runtime and malformed URL distinguish unreachable/configuration states.
- [ ] Rapidly switch workspace while first probe is delayed; old success cannot mark new target connected.
- [ ] Valid connection shows correct verified identity without secret leakage.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Green connected status requires a successful current authenticated probe.
- [ ] Configuration and live health are distinct.
- [ ] Wrong-workspace screenshot scenario no longer shows a success indicator.

#### Non-goals and cautions

Preserve server allowlisting and existing separate local/session storage policy unless a security-reviewed change is required. Do not touch base/ui.

#### Sources

- [S10: browser connection storage/configured predicate](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/apps/web/src/lib/connection.ts)
- [S11: header connection status and connection dialog activation](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/apps/web/src/routes/%2Blayout.svelte)
- [S12: connection form saves settings without a probe](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/apps/web/src/lib/components/ConnectionDialog.svelte)

<a id="f09"></a>
### F09 — Separate failed loads from empty collections and invalidate stale workspace data

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#15](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/15) (QA F09)

#### Summary

Approvals displayed 0 pending and No actions are waiting for approval at the same time as a workspace load error.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F09 |
| Priority | P2 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | web / state management |
| Evidence | OBSERVED false empty message; CODE-CONFIRMED page load/state behavior |
| Historical test cases | T63, T64, T65 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

The wrong-workspace screenshot visibly combined a red denial banner with an empty-state assurance. Pages required manual refresh after changing connection settings.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

Approvals starts with an empty array and renders empty based on length after loading=false even if load failed. Data fetching is tied to onMount/manual refresh rather than a scoped connection generation; automation has similar patterns.

#### Impact

Failure to retrieve state is misrepresented as evidence there is no work. Connection changes may leave old workspace data visible/actionable unless invalidated.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Represent loading, success-empty, success-data and failure separately. Pending/job counts are unknown on failure rather than zero.
2. Key page data and in-flight requests by the active connection generation/workspace. Clear or visibly mark stale data when settings change.
3. Abort old requests or reject their responses if connection key no longer matches; prevent actions on stale rows.
4. Reload relevant read-only data after a verified connection change; do not rely only on remounting or manual refresh.
5. Preserve error messages and offer retry; do not clear evidence of failure by filtering arrays.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] 401, workspace denial, offline server and 500 each render load failure without a no-work assurance.
- [ ] Successful empty array alone shows zero/no pending.
- [ ] Switch workspace during delayed approvals/automation load and assert no old items or old success response populate the new context.
- [ ] Controls cannot act on stale data during reconnect.
- [ ] Recovering request updates both count and table correctly.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Error and empty states are mutually accurate.
- [ ] Connection changes invalidate stale workspace data and actions.
- [ ] Tests reproduce the exact misleading screenshot and verify corrected behavior.

#### Non-goals and cautions

Keep this separate from deciding which backend policies allow a workspace. It is client state correctness, not an allowlist relaxation.

#### Related QA work

QA F08. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

<a id="f10"></a>
### F10 — Add semantic previews for scheduled-job changes and skill publishing

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#16](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/16) (QA F10)

#### Summary

Scheduling approvals expose an opaque action name and a fingerprint but show No action-specific preview is available by default.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F10 |
| Priority | P2 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | approvals / usability / review |
| Evidence | OBSERVED generic previews; CODE-CONFIRMED supported preview types |
| Historical test cases | T64, T67, T69, T40 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

Create, pause and resume screens required Grant/Reject decisions without an immediately readable summary of target, prompt, service, schedule or enabled-state change. Raw normalized JSON was expandable.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

ApprovalItem specializes filesystem.write and process.spawn; scheduling and publishing fall through to a generic preview. The action arguments are available, so this is a presentation/review gap rather than missing backend approval.

#### Impact

Users are encouraged to approve unfamiliar action identifiers without understanding effect. A fingerprint verifies identity but is not a human explanation.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Render schedule.job.create with job identity, prompt, service/owner, classified data, bounds, enabled/idempotent settings and readable date/time/timezone.
2. Render schedule.job.update as an explicit before/after diff with revision, enabled→paused/resumed and any other changed fields. Use immutable preview data bound to the approved action, not unrelated current data.
3. Add skill.publish preview showing name, version, source run/draft and content digest with a safe bounded preview and review semantics.
4. Retain normalized JSON, capability scopes and fingerprint for detailed inspection. Validate malformed/missing arguments with an honest fallback, not guessed values.
5. Label the decision specifically (for example approve pause) while leaving the API grant value unchanged. Never imply granted means executed before persistence confirmation.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Fixture snapshots for create/pause/resume and skill publish render correct targets, limits, before/after changes and source digests.
- [ ] Malformed arguments fall back visibly without crashing or fabricating a safe summary.
- [ ] Content is escaped; prompts cannot inject markup.
- [ ] Desktop/mobile, keyboard, screen-reader and long prompt/ID cases remain usable.
- [ ] Preview shown corresponds to the exact fingerprint submitted.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Scheduling decisions no longer rely on generic preview alone.
- [ ] Pause/resume direction and effect are immediately clear.
- [ ] Approval semantics, raw inspection and accessibility are preserved.

#### Non-goals and cautions

No redesign or merge from base/ui. Do not fetch sensitive contents into the preview without current workspace policy.

- [S14: semantic approval previews, expiration label and buttons](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/apps/web/src/lib/components/ApprovalItem.svelte)

<a id="f11"></a>
### F11 — Audit and surface excluded reviewed skills instead of silently skipping them

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#17](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/17) (QA F11)

#### Summary

Tamper rejection works, but enabled/reviewed skills can disappear from a run with no explicit exclusion reason.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F11 |
| Priority | P2 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | skills / integrity / observability |
| Evidence | OBSERVED skill omission without reason; CODE-CONFIRMED silent path |
| Historical test cases | T41, T43, T46, T48 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

After appending one newline, skill e587475f... stopped appearing in loaded_skills; the run still recorded completed success and no explicit skill-integrity event.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

load_reviewed_skill_context returns None for unreviewed/wrong-workspace records, missing source, oversize or digest mismatch. Caller skips them and continues.

#### Impact

An automation can silently stop following an expected procedure while presenting success. Excluding an optional invalid skill may be correct, but absence alone is poor operational evidence.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Return a typed loading outcome with metadata/reason (loaded, not_found, digest_mismatch, oversize, not_eligible, read_error) rather than flattening all exclusions into None.
2. Emit a safe run-correlated audit/diagnostic event for relevant enabled skills rejected by integrity checks. Include ID/version and expected/observed digest where safe, not raw source or secret contents.
3. Show a warning/integrity status in Skills and affected run views; do not keep a rejected artifact looking simply healthy/enabled.
4. Define required versus optional skill behavior explicitly. Required missing/corrupt procedure should block before provider dispatch; optional exclusion may continue with visible warning.
5. Restore eligibility only after matching reviewed source is restored or a new revision is reviewed through the existing approval path.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Valid source loads with exact digest metadata.
- [ ] One-byte/newline mutation yields digest_mismatch and no source injection.
- [ ] Missing and oversized source yield distinct safe reasons.
- [ ] Optional exclusion remains visible; required exclusion blocks provider call.
- [ ] No raw skill content or known secret reaches exclusion log.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Every relevant rejection can be explained from run/skill diagnostics.
- [ ] Required-skill policy is explicit and tested.
- [ ] Existing digest rejection is retained and cannot be bypassed by warnings.

#### Non-goals and cautions

No evidence that the modified skill entered model context was found. Do not call successful tamper exclusion a security failure; fix silent degradation.

#### Related QA work

QA F12. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

<a id="f12"></a>
### F12 — Bound skill source reads before allocation and decoding

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#18](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/18) (QA F12)

#### Summary

The 65,536-byte skill size check occurs only after reading the complete file into a String.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F12 |
| Priority | P1 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | skills / resource limits |
| Evidence | CODE-CONFIRMED late size check; oversized artifact not manually tested |
| Historical test cases | T45, T46, T48 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

Our test changed one newline; it did not exercise a large source or memory pressure from skills. The code uses tokio::fs::read_to_string followed by source.len() > 65_536.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

An acceptance limit after full read does not cap I/O/allocation. A very large persisted artifact can consume substantial resources before being rejected.

#### Impact

Digest protection can prevent use of changed bytes while still allowing resource exhaustion during inspection. No observed VmmemWSL spike is attributed to this path.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Use a bounded async reader that reads at most MAX_SKILL_BYTES+1, rejects overflow and then decodes/hash-checks only bounded bytes.
2. A metadata length precheck can reject obvious oversize files but must not replace the bounded read because contents can change after stat.
3. Preserve exact byte hashing and UTF-8/error semantics; no normalization of whitespace before digest comparison.
4. Avoid following unbounded special streams; preserve/establish appropriate regular-file and containment checks as the existing artifact model permits.
5. Return typed exclusion/error so integrity diagnostics distinguish oversize, malformed encoding and digest mismatch.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Exactly at limit, one byte above, much larger file and file growth between metadata/read.
- [ ] Invalid UTF-8, read permission error, missing file and cancellation during bounded read.
- [ ] Reader instrumentation proves no more than limit+1 bytes consumed; do not allocate a multi-GB fixture merely to test the bound.
- [ ] Original digest test and restored valid file still work.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Memory and bytes read are bounded before whole-file allocation.
- [ ] Oversize is rejected independently of metadata races.
- [ ] No weakening of digest or containment checks.

#### Non-goals and cautions

Keep this focused on the confirmed skill-loader read, not speculative machine-wide memory leaks.

#### Related QA work

QA F11. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

- [S02: runtime composition, scheduler, skill loading, finalization](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/crates/lumen-cli/src/runtime.rs)

<a id="f13"></a>
### F13 — Validate meaningful workflow capture and reuse beyond empty provenance drafts

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#19](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/19) (QA F13)

#### Summary

The captured “skill” contained no actions and generic placeholders; successful storage/publishing was mistakenly described as completed functional learning.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F13 |
| Priority | P2 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | skills / workflow capture / acceptance |
| Evidence | MANUAL VALIDATION GAP; CODE-CONFIRMED capture-template limitations |
| Historical test cases | T38, T39, T40, T41, T43 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

Draft b33e318d... had Actions none, only run_created/run_completed audit kinds, operator-review placeholder, generic completed expected output and no captured failures.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

capture_workflow_draft builds Markdown from action kinds/states/argument hashes and audit-event names. It does not automatically reconstruct reusable arguments, required inputs or a tested procedure.

#### Impact

Users may mistake an evidence summary for a validated reusable workflow. Empty provenance is useful for traceability but not evidence a future agent can repeat a task.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Define and document the distinction between a captured provenance draft, operator-edited procedure and approved reusable skill. Do not label the first as learned automation.
2. Capture a real bounded tool-bearing run after F01. Preserve ordered action provenance while redacting/classifying data; hashes alone must not be presented as executable inputs.
3. Add an explicit review/completeness step for required variables, expected effects, limits and failure handling; incomplete drafts may remain drafts with visible warnings.
4. Decide how an operator supplies reusable procedure content through supported editing/review paths; avoid inventing automatic task-learning scope for this bugfix.
5. Re-use the reviewed skill on a second independent fixture and verify intended effects/results with normal authorization.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] A no-action run is clearly labeled provenance-only/incomplete, not advertised as validated reusable procedure.
- [ ] Completed multi-action source capture has exact run/workspace-scoped provenance and no unrelated audit rows.
- [ ] Secret redaction and incomplete/failed source rejection remain intact.
- [ ] An edited/reviewed procedure works on a new nonce/path within authorized boundaries.
- [ ] Tampered published source stays excluded.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Capture/publish success and meaningful reuse success are separately reported.
- [ ] One nontrivial reusable workflow has reproducible input/output and normal authorization evidence.
- [ ] The UI/docs do not overstate empty drafts.

#### Non-goals and cautions

Preserve the repaired >1,000-event and cross-workspace capture checks in PR #6. Do not duplicate broader orchestration #4.

QA F01, QA F11, QA F22. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

### F14 — Bound graceful shutdown with open SSE streams and active runs

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#20](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/20) (QA F14)

#### Summary

The runtime awaits HTTP graceful shutdown before cancelling its service/scheduler, while SSE subscriptions have no explicit shutdown termination.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F14 |
| Priority | P1 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | runtime / shutdown / streaming |
| Evidence | CODE-REVIEW RISK; operator termination was uncertain, no hang reproduced |
| Historical test cases | T24, T49, T70, T71 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

Operator saw only ^C, closed the terminal and believed servers stopped. No process/port exit evidence was captured. WSL was later shut down to reclaim memory.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

serve awaits axum::serve(...).with_graceful_shutdown(shutdown_signal()) before service.shutdown(). The SSE broker unfold waits for events without a shutdown token or terminal completion rule. An open subscription may delay HTTP drain and therefore delay service cancellation.

#### Impact

Shutdown may wait indefinitely with open event streams, while the scheduler cancellation/cleanup has not yet run. This is a testable structural risk, not a proven explanation for the observed ^C output.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Define a shared shutdown coordinator: stop accepting new work, cancel new scheduling immediately, cancel or deliberately drain active runs, close SSE readers and enforce an overall bounded deadline.
2. Thread shutdown cancellation through SSE subscription state and wake receivers on termination. Document whether terminal run events close their streams or require client closure.
3. Persist appropriate terminal/unknown state before teardown where possible; avoid pretending cancellation guarantees no external side effect.
4. Close database only after bounded service cleanup; expose first shutdown signal, draining and final stopped message. Handle second interrupt explicitly.
5. Do not rely on shell window closure or wsl --shutdown as application lifecycle proof.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Hold an idle SSE client open, send Ctrl+C/SIGINT and prove listener closes and process exits within budget.
- [ ] Repeat with active generation, executing sandboxed process and awaiting approval.
- [ ] No scheduled model call is started after shutdown requested.
- [ ] Disconnected browser, reconnect loop and second interrupt cannot keep runtime alive indefinitely.
- [ ] Restart same DB after forced deadline and reconcile unknown/interrupted work.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Deterministic regression reproduces or disproves the suspected SSE ordering issue.
- [ ] Shutdown has an enforced upper bound and clear exit evidence.
- [ ] Scheduler, run state and DB teardown follow documented order.

#### Non-goals and cautions

Do not assert shell closure always kills descendants. Keep Windows signals/platform behavior explicit and retain kernel sandbox limitations.

#### Related QA work

QA F04, QA F22. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

#### Sources

- [S07: CLI command dispatch, serve and shutdown signal](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/crates/lumen-cli/src/lib.rs)
- [S08: SSE broker and subscription lifecycle](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/crates/lumen-server/src/sse.rs)
- [S02: runtime composition, scheduler, skill loading, finalization](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/crates/lumen-cli/src/runtime.rs)

<a id="f15"></a>
### F15 — Provide a shell-aware, resumable functional-test launcher

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#21](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/21) (QA F15)

#### Summary

Manual acceptance depended on variables and remembered shell context across three terminals, causing repeated avoidable failures.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F15 |
| Priority | P2 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | developer experience / QA tooling |
| Evidence | OBSERVED repeated environment/paste failures |
| Historical test cases | T08, T10, T21, T22, T29, T30, T54, T59, T60, T61, T63 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

Bash export/awk were twice attempted in PowerShell; token/workspace variables were lost/malformed; stale browser workspace caused denial; test fixture disappeared and was recreated.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

No single tested entry point selected runtime root, shell, binary, workspace, ports and connection configuration. Instructions assumed exports in one terminal applied to another.

#### Impact

Test results become hard to reproduce and false failures obscure product bugs. Careless helper automation could also grant unrelated approvals or delete the wrong tree.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Add documented PowerShell entry point that explicitly invokes the chosen WSL2 distro and Bash entry point for the Linux runtime; fail on wrong shell/platform rather than guessing.
2. Create/reuse an explicitly selected, guarded disposable fixture root on the Linux filesystem. Persist nonsecret manifest: repo SHA/dirty status, binary identity, workspace ID, config path and owned process IDs.
3. Read the workspace from validated config instead of regenerating on each resume. Show current workspace and safe connection instructions; keep token in local protected storage/session environment and out of logs/URLs.
4. Provide start/status/stop/resume helpers with bounded waits and authenticated readiness checks. Never terminate unrelated Ollama, Docker, WSL distros or developer jobs.
5. Approval helpers must select exactly one expected run/action, check expiry and prompt for actual approval when appropriate; only explicitly disposable deterministic tests may automate decisions.
6. Use fail-visible HTTP handling and copy/paste blocks that do not require manual escaped JSON editing.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Fresh PowerShell terminal → chosen WSL test fixture without export/awk failures.
- [ ] New Bash terminal resumes same workspace/token source; stale browser identity gets a clear correction.
- [ ] Missing config, missing tools, occupied unrelated port and wrong worktree fail safely.
- [ ] Repeated start/stop does not duplicate services or regenerate UUID.
- [ ] No tokens or raw sensitive prompt data in report/process command log.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Documented launcher reproduces the setup in a fresh shell.
- [ ] Resume preserves selected test identity and process ownership.
- [ ] No changes to base/ui or unrelated checkout.

#### Non-goals and cautions

No global machine reconfiguration or automatic app installation without explicit scope. Snapshot/destructive fixture support is tracked separately.

QA F16, QA F17, QA F18, QA F19. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

<a id="f16"></a>
### F16 — Make sandbox diagnostics config-independent and document readiness/startup status

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#22](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/22) (QA F16)

#### Summary

Basic sandbox capability inspection unnecessarily requires lumen.toml, and the CLI starts serving without a useful readiness summary.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F16 |
| Priority | P2 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | CLI / diagnostics / readiness |
| Evidence | OBSERVED missing-config diagnostic failure and nonexistent /health; CODE-CONFIRMED dispatch order |
| Historical test cases | T09, T11, T24, T25, T26, T55, T70 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

sandbox report compiled but failed missing lumen.toml. Authenticated /health returned 404 because the endpoint does not exist. serve printed only Cargo launch output.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

Config::load and directory preparation happen before command dispatch. No documented health route was used; authentication middleware runs before the 404 route result. Runtime logs do not guide operators to a valid readiness request.

#### Impact

First-time setup cannot inspect prerequisites without configuring the application it is trying to diagnose. Users mistake silent processes or unauthorized requests for health.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Dispatch purely host-level sandbox report before project config/DB/key-store initialization where safe; support structured output and meaningful unavailable reasons.
2. Separate liveness, authenticated workspace readiness and model-provider health. Choose/document actual routes rather than assuming /health; an existing capabilities request may serve part of this purpose.
3. Print a sanitized startup summary: listening address, active workspace, config path, sandbox backend/strength and readiness state. Never print token or secrets.
4. Report orderly stopping/stopped and actionable startup failures without suggesting privilege expansion.
5. Keep startup refusal on a broken audit chain and required unavailable sandbox; diagnostics must not accidentally bootstrap directories/DB as a side effect.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] sandbox report works from a directory without lumen.toml and without DB setup.
- [ ] Unavailable Windows kernel sandbox is honestly reported, not silently downgraded.
- [ ] Documented readiness endpoint yields expected auth/workspace/error behavior.
- [ ] Corrupted audit state still prevents serving.
- [ ] Startup/shutdown logs contain no secrets.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Host capability reporting has no unnecessary project prerequisites.
- [ ] Operators have one documented, testable readiness path.
- [ ] CLI visibly distinguishes starting, ready and stopped.

#### Non-goals and cautions

No need to add an unauthenticated endpoint exposing workspace data. Correct the test documentation even if endpoint names remain unchanged.

#### Related QA work

QA F08, QA F14. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.
