<a id="f17"></a>
### F17 — Document audit pagination bounds and make QA HTTP failures visible

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#23](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/23) (QA F17)

#### Summary

The acceptance instructions requested audit limit=250 and piped error JSON into .events[], obscuring the real validation error.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F17 |
| Priority | P2 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | API / audit / QA scripts |
| Evidence | OBSERVED invalid test command; CODE-CONFIRMED 1–200 limit |
| Historical test cases | T25, T26, T29, T30, T43, T47, T48 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

jq reported Cannot iterate over null. Reducing to 100 returned expected events. Source rejects after<0, limit=0 and limit>200 with invalid audit page bounds.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

Test commands did not inspect HTTP status or error envelopes before iterating result arrays. They also fetched from after=0 repeatedly instead of carrying the last sequence.

#### Impact

An API validation response appears to be a broken audit subsystem. Larger test histories may miss later events if the first page is repeatedly reused.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Document valid after and limit ranges, defaults and exclusive sequence-cursor semantics based on implementation; state how to retrieve the next page.
2. Add a shared QA request helper that captures status/body, has connect/total deadlines, displays error.code/message and refuses success assertions on non-2xx.
3. Validate response shape before jq iteration. Do not use .events[]? or //[] to hide error objects as an empty success.
4. Paginate or use run-scoped repository/API capabilities where supported. Stop when last page is exhausted and avoid unbounded repeated reads.
5. Test API error envelopes for malformed numeric values, invalid UUID, unauthorized token and forbidden workspace, documenting HTTP and machine-readable codes.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] limit 0, 1, 100, 200, 201, 250; negative after; malformed numbers; missing defaults.
- [ ] More than one page of events: run-specific lookup does not incorrectly declare absence after first 100.
- [ ] 401/403/400/500 each produce a useful failing diagnostic rather than jq null error.
- [ ] No secret header is echoed in failed-command logs.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Examples never use unsupported page bounds.
- [ ] Failure responses remain visible and yield failure status.
- [ ] Paged evidence captures all relevant events without masking absence.

#### Non-goals and cautions

This corrects our test command; rejecting limit=250 is not a backend bug. Do not enlarge limits merely to make the bad command pass.

#### Related QA work

QA F15. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

<a id="f18"></a>
### F18 — Isolate Windows/WSL native dependencies and stabilize QA build caches

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#24](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/24) (QA F18), [#25](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/25) (QA F19)

#### Summary

Windows/WSL development reused a worktree dependency environment and alternated Cargo target directories, producing missing native bindings and expensive rebuilds.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F18 |
| Priority | P2 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | developer experience / build portability |
| Evidence | OBSERVED missing native binding and repeated rebuilds; causes partly inferred |
| Historical test cases | T09, T23, T24, T49, T52, T56, T57, T58 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

Vite failed on @rolldown/binding-linux-x64-gnu; reinstall from WSL fixed startup. Cargo paths switched from /tmp/lumen-functional-target to worktree target, and simple commands rebuilt large crates for 47.84s, 1m57s, 1m40s, 1m18s and 1m00s across shown runs.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

Native packages are platform-specific; environment/caches need explicit OS boundaries. Target-directory changes explain some rebuilds, but remaining invalidation was not diagnosed. npm run dev itself is not the defect.

#### Impact

Iteration becomes slow and memory-heavy; deletion/reinstall fixes one OS while potentially breaking the other environment. Uncontrolled lockfile changes could hide portability regressions.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Document supported Linux/Windows dependency installations and use separate worktrees or tested architecture-aware package configuration. Respect pnpm lockfile and pinned package-manager version.
2. Give QA a stable Linux-side Cargo target directory separated from Windows output and large /mnt/c metadata overhead where practical; do not delete caches on every run.
3. Build the selected binary once and execute it for migrate/report/verify/serve, recording SHA/features/binary hash.
4. Investigate unexplained recompilation using Cargo fingerprint diagnostics and environment comparison; record causes rather than blaming Rust generally.
5. Check git status/diff before/after install; document failed native package detection with a safe remedy, not broad deletion of unrelated node_modules or lockfiles.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Fresh WSL install resolves Linux native dependencies with frozen lockfile; Windows remains independently functional.
- [ ] Repeated unchanged CLI commands reuse built binary.
- [ ] Switching environments does not reuse incompatible native artifacts.
- [ ] Expected code/features/toolchain changes invalidate cache intentionally.
- [ ] Track cold versus warm startup separately; no unsupported performance guarantee.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] OS-specific dependencies are isolated or explicitly supported.
- [ ] Acceptance command loop does not unnecessarily rebuild.
- [ ] Source/lockfile diffs are inspected and not silently discarded.

#### Non-goals and cautions

No global npm/pnpm upgrade, Windows driver changes, or modification of base/ui. Preserve original Windows checkout.

#### Related QA work

QA F15, QA F23. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

- [S17: web scripts and frontend dependencies](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/apps/web/package.json)
- [X04: Cargo build cache / target directory](https://doc.rust-lang.org/cargo/reference/build-cache.html)
- [X05: pnpm workspace settings](https://pnpm.io/settings)

### F19 — Snapshot destructive QA fixtures and preserve evidence before cleanup

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#21](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/21) (QA F15)

#### Summary

Skill and audit tamper tests modified the only disposable fixture, and deletion afterward destroyed the DB and forced a new workspace identity.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F19 |
| Priority | P2 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | QA / evidence / fixtures |
| Evidence | OBSERVED evidence-destructive cleanup and unguarded mutation workflow |
| Historical test cases | T21, T45, T46, T49, T50, T51, T52, T53, T54 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

One newline changed skill digest; sequence 28 payload was overwritten; verifier/startup refused corruption. No healthy snapshot or modified-row count was captured. rm -rf then removed the complete runtime.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

Test procedure relied on a volatile fixed /tmp directory, hard-coded sequence and broad cleanup rather than named snapshots, fixture ownership and retained artifacts.

#### Impact

Useful negative tests become hard to reproduce or inspect. A copied command against a different path could mutate the wrong row or silently create a new empty database.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Mark each fixture with an ownership manifest and refuse destructive operations outside its resolved root. Validate real path, expected workspace, table/schema and target record before mutation.
2. Use SQLite backup API for a consistent healthy DB snapshot including WAL effects; copy/hash required skill artifacts separately. Do not copy only a live main DB file.
3. Run tamper mutations against a clone. Assert exactly one targeted row changed and retain before/after payload/digests safely.
4. Archive command outcomes, expected negative results, logs, source SHA and snapshot hashes before teardown. Redact credentials.
5. Restore verified healthy fixture for subsequent UI/restart tests without silently regenerating workspace; separate delete-test-fixture from stop-process.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Missing source DB fails with read-only/existing-file mode rather than creating an empty DB.
- [ ] WAL-active backup restores and verifies consistently.
- [ ] Tamper mutation affects exactly the intended event and leaves source healthy snapshot unchanged.
- [ ] Cleanup refuses root, arbitrary user directory, symlink escape and unowned fixture.
- [ ] Restored fixture preserves jobs, skills and workspace identity.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Every destructive test has a recoverable healthy starting point and archived evidence.
- [ ] Test metadata survives stop/restart and controlled cleanup.
- [ ] No raw credentials or unrelated user files are included.

#### Non-goals and cautions

Do not “repair” corruption by recalculating trusted hashes. Keep intentional tamper detection as expected pass, not red/failed suite noise.

#### Related QA work

QA F15, QA F22. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

#### Sources

- [S07: CLI command dispatch, serve and shutdown signal](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/blob/7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94/crates/lumen-cli/src/lib.rs)

<a id="f20"></a>
### F20 — Verify and expose GPU-required policy for managed local inference

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#28](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/28) (QA F20)

#### Summary

GPU acceleration worked for qwen3:4b at sampled times, but no evidence established the requested visible failure on CPU-only fallback across managed requests/restarts.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F20 |
| Priority | P2 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | models / GPU / operator diagnostics |
| Evidence | OBSERVED one GPU-resident model; universal enforcement UNVERIFIED |
| Historical test cases | T16, T17, T18, T19, T20, T44, T71 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

Post-fix ollama ps reported 100% GPU, 3.2GB/context4096; NVIDIA showed GPU allocation. User reported an external agent finished machine configuration; its script inventory, official Ollama version and GPU-failure behavior were never attached.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

Residency telemetry is not an enforced policy and cannot prove attribution to a given run. Lumen cannot guarantee device selection for arbitrary unrelated tools started elsewhere on a machine.

#### Impact

Users may unintentionally perform expensive CPU-only inference while believing a previous GPU sample establishes permanent enforcement.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Define scope explicitly: Lumen-managed provider requests and any separately managed launcher, not every program on the OS. Document unsupported runtimes.
2. Represent runtime/model/backend/device, telemetry timestamp and execution mode (GPU, mixed offload, CPU-only, unavailable/unknown). Never infer full-GPU from response speed or total system utilization.
3. Add a requested GPU-required provider policy or validate the existing external wrapper, failing visibly on CPU-only/unknown according to policy. A preflight alone is not a proof of future runtime behavior; document detection/enforcement limits.
4. Test both CLI and OpenAI-compatible API paths actually routed through the managed boundary, plus model reload/service restart.
5. Keep local/remote trust and data-class policy separate from GPU capability. No silent remote fallback or driver surgery.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Known fitting model reports GPU under managed request with safe provider/run correlation.
- [ ] Controlled CPU-only or telemetry-unavailable condition is rejected/flagged as configured.
- [ ] Mixed CPU/GPU offload is distinguished and requires explicit policy allowance.
- [ ] Restart/new shell/model change does not silently drop policy.
- [ ] Unmanaged direct runtime access is honestly outside enforcement coverage.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] GPU-required guarantees are precisely scoped and tested, not universal marketing.
- [ ] Observed residency and enforcement status are separately displayed.

<a id="f21"></a>
### F21 — Diagnose WSL memory pressure and document safe resource controls

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#26](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/26) (QA F21)

#### Summary

The operator ended the session by shutting down WSL because VmmemWSL was consuming too much host RAM; no attribution or memory baseline was retained.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F21 |
| Priority | P2 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | environment / WSL / diagnostics |
| Evidence | USER-REPORTED pressure; root cause UNVERIFIED |
| Historical test cases | T16, T20, T23, T24, T49, T52, T71 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

Repeated Rust builds and a local model ran in WSL. GPU residency was observed, but host RAM usage was not measured. wsl --shutdown was instructed and completion acknowledged.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

Possible contributors include application RSS, compiler processes, model runtime host allocations, page cache and other WSL workloads. None was measured sufficiently to diagnose a Lumen leak.

#### Impact

Development is disrupted and shutdown destroys volatile test state. An arbitrary RAM cap can cause avoidable OOMs or harm unrelated workloads.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Add a nonprivileged diagnostic recipe/optional harness capture for host physical RAM, WSL distro/version, free/available/cache, top process RSS and relevant runtime/build processes.
2. Compare baseline, build, model-load, generation, idle, model-unload and stopped-server samples. Keep GPU memory separate from host memory.
3. Inspect existing .wslconfig/wsl.conf before suggesting supported memory/reclamation options; use current Microsoft guidance, explain distro versus VM scope and retain rollback.
4. Do not automatically shut down every WSL distro, modify driver settings or pick a fixed cap without measured host capacity and user consent.
5. Document controlled WSL shutdown as an environment action that stops all distro processes, not as the app graceful-stop mechanism.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Diagnostic capture redacts unrelated process command-line secrets.
- [ ] A measured example distinguishes process RSS from reclaimable cache.
- [ ] Any configuration adjustment is reversible, version-compatible and tested for model/build OOM regressions.
- [ ] Do not file/close a memory leak fix without attributable measurements.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Root cause remains explicitly unknown until measurements support a conclusion.
- [ ] A reproducible before/during/after diagnostic and safe configuration decision procedure exists.
- [ ] No unrequested host-wide settings changes occur.

#### Non-goals and cautions

This is an environment/documentation follow-up, not a proven runtime defect or a default reason to block PR #6 code.

#### Related QA work

QA F18, QA F19, QA F20. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

- [X02: Ollama FAQ: model residency and runtime behavior](https://docs.ollama.com/faq)

<a id="f22"></a>
### F22 — Close manual acceptance gaps for failure, denial, rejection and same-DB restart

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#8](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/8) (QA F02)

#### Summary

The manual pass covered positive administration and selected tamper checks but was prematurely labeled full functional acceptance.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F22 |
| Priority | P1 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | QA / integration / release gate |
| Evidence | MANUAL COVERAGE GAP; some automated coverage already reported |
| Historical test cases | T05, T07, T28, T38, T39, T42, T43, T53, T54, T67, T69, T70 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

No live tool round trip/final answer, forbidden zero-grant action, rejected pause/resume, same-DB restart continuity, occurrence-row terminal check, provider outage or bounded shutdown was completed.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

Test assertions focused on receipts/lifecycle/UI flags. The fixture was deleted rather than restarted. Agent-reported automated suites should not be counted as live manual coverage of paths they may mock.

#### Impact

A pass summary can exceed its evidence and allow lifecycle/durability defects through review.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Maintain a versioned acceptance matrix with pass/fail/inconclusive/not-run/expected-refusal statuses, source commit and evidence path for each assertion.
2. Add deterministic negative tests for zero-grant service actions, grant/reject/replay/expiry, unavailable provider, timeout, cancellation and injected storage failure.
3. Restart the same healthy fixture before due, after claim/reservation, after completion and while awaiting approval; inspect persisted job/run/occurrence/audit/skill state.
4. For non-idempotent unknown outcomes require explicit reconciliation; never promise exactly-once external side effects based only on a unique key.
5. Exercise the browser Reject flow in both directions, refresh and verify revision/state did not change. Add live returned-text/tool assertions from F01.
6. Preserve existing automated security tests and document mocks versus real provider/kernel/desktop coverage. Block closure on unsupported broad claims.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Zero-grant forbidden action causes no side effect before or after approval attempts.
- [ ] Rejected pause leaves enabled/revision unchanged; rejected resume leaves paused/revision unchanged.
- [ ] Same DB restart retains published skill and approved future job; completed one-shot does not re-execute accidentally.
- [ ] Failure/timeout/cancel states agree across API/UI/audit/DB.
- [ ] Corrupted chain startup remains denied and no approval/security assertion weakened.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Each missing manual assertion has executed evidence or an explicit owner-approved deferral.
- [ ] No full acceptance claim while P1 integrity gaps remain unverified.
- [ ] Evidence maps to exact SHA and fixture rather than reconstructed memory.

#### Non-goals and cautions

This is a test gate, not a request to implement unrelated roadmap milestones. No merge authorization is implied by completing the matrix.

#### Related QA work

QA F01, QA F02, QA F03, QA F04, QA F07, QA F14, QA F19, QA F23. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

### F23 — Reconcile test inventory/counts and record complete platform verification scope

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#27](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/27) (QA F22)

#### Summary

The initial Windows report said 264 workspace tests passed; the later final-head report said 259. Full Linux desktop verification remained blocked by missing native prerequisites.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F23 |
| Priority | P1 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | QA / CI / portability |
| Evidence | REPORTED totals differ; Linux desktop explicitly NOT VERIFIED |
| Historical test cases | T03, T04, T05, T06, T07 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

Final report lists 259 Windows tests, 12 Vitest, 14 Playwright, and 126 WSL tests across four commands. It states no assertions were weakened and no tests ignored. An earlier wider WSL run had a two-second timeout followed by isolated success.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

Totals lack an attached machine-readable per-test inventory tied to SHA/platform/features. Platform gating and refactoring may explain changes; neither weakening nor equivalent coverage can be inferred from totals alone.

#### Impact

Contributors cannot independently reconcile what was executed versus compiled/gated/blocked. A Linux sandbox CI pass can be mistaken for full Linux desktop acceptance.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Generate per-suite inventory including test name, target, platform, features, ignored/gated status and result tied to git SHA/toolchain/command.
2. Compare initial repair revision against 7c3080a and explain the 264→259 difference by named additions/removals/renames/platform gates. Preserve substantive security assertions.
3. Run Linux desktop verification in an environment with required GTK/pkg-config/native dependencies or maintain an explicit blocked status with owner and exact scope.
4. Reproduce or clearly retain the historical two-second parallel timeout as an unresolved flaky-test observation until the test identity/log can be obtained. Do not simply enlarge timeout.
5. Store raw logs and CI job links separately from summaries; scope counts to exact commands and distinguish user-executed, agent-reported and CI-verified evidence.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Inventory totals reconcile to reported command totals.
- [ ] Windows/Unix-specific tests are mapped to executed supported environments.
- [ ] Full desktop verification is either evidenced or visibly excluded.
- [ ] No disabled test or relaxed assertion masquerades as reconciliation.
- [ ] A rerun on the final commit has reproducible report artifacts.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Count change has a named-test explanation or is explicitly unresolved.
- [ ] Platform verification statement precisely matches evidence.
- [ ] Report no longer uses one green CI check as proxy for all targets.

#### Non-goals and cautions

This is not an accusation that tests were weakened. Existing report explicitly denies weakening; verify with inventories rather than inventing a cause.

#### Related QA work

QA F18, QA F22. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

### F24 — Render human-readable automation schedules and remove duplicate desktop labels

**Canonical GitHub issue(s):** [#16](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/issues/16) (QA F10)

#### Summary

Automation renders millisecond epoch schedule/next-due values and repeats desktop table labels inside every row.

#### Classification and scope

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Report observation | F24 |
| Priority | P2 (triage recommendation, not a CVSS score) |
| Area | web / automation presentation |
| Evidence | OBSERVED screenshots and CODE-CONFIRMED rendering |
| Historical test cases | T65, T66, T67, T68, T69 |
| Baseline | PR #6, `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94` |

#### Observed behavior and evidence limits

Screens showed once at 1789261284948, next 1789261284948, duplicated Prompt/Schedule/Service and identity/owner/grants headings; singular counts displayed as 1 jobs, 1 service identities and 1 actions.

#### Mechanism / root-cause analysis

scheduleText interpolates raw run_at/start_at/interval_millis; next_due_at is printed directly. Row field-label spans are always present while desktop header is also shown; mobile CSS only hides table header.

#### Impact

State transitions can be correct but difficult to interpret. Raw millisecond timestamps contributed to confusion about when work should run.

#### Proposed implementation

1. Use shared locale/timezone-aware formatting for dates/times and readable interval durations, keeping raw epoch and exact timezone available in details/tooltips for debugging.
2. Show no next occurrence versus paused/completed distinctly; do not claim enabled alone means work is scheduled or has succeeded.
3. Hide redundant row labels at desktop/table breakpoints while retaining accessible column associations and mobile card labels.
4. Correct singular/plural counts and retain readable long prompt/ID behavior.
5. Preserve tested approval-pending state: display changes only after actual approved mutation/refresh, never optimistic enablement.

#### Reproduction and regression plan

The historical observations above are not a claim that every case below has been executed. Use an owned disposable fixture and reproduce static risks deterministically before claiming a runtime failure.

- [ ] Once and interval schedules, null next_due, different locale/timezone, date rollover and daylight-saving display cases.
- [ ] Desktop/table and narrow/mobile views have no redundant visual labels and retain accessible context.
- [ ] One vs multiple jobs/identities/actions use correct text.
- [ ] Pending pause/resume screenshots still show persisted preapproval state.
- [ ] No behavior changes to schedule values or revision arithmetic.

#### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Dates/durations are intelligible without external epoch conversion.
- [ ] Desktop and mobile labels are appropriate and accessible.
- [ ] No scheduling/approval behavior changed as part of presentation fix.

#### Non-goals and cautions

Small polish in current functional UI only; do not touch or import base/ui.

#### Related QA work

QA F10. See the QA report/index for corresponding issue numbers.

## Outstanding acceptance matrix

This is **future work**, not an additional set of passing tests. A checked receipt or prompt submitted to an LLM does not satisfy a side-effect assertion. Fixtures must be synthetic and local; mocks should be identified.

| Gate | Scenario | Required evidence | Current status | Related findings |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| G01 | Live text response | Actual run.completed text/result, run/provider correlation, assertion result | Not checked | F01, F20 |
| G02 | Real model-driven read | Random file nonce absent from prompt, authorized action record, exact answer | Not run | F01 |
| G03 | Model-driven write grant | File absent before grant; exact normalized action; file bytes afterward | Not run manually | F01, F22 |
| G04 | Write reject | No file modification, denied/rejected state and terminal handling | Not run manually | F01, F22 |
| G05 | Zero-grant service forbidden action | No tool dispatch/side effect, denial cause and job/run attribution | Not run | F01, F22 |
| G06 | Scheduled aggregate quotas | Same policy limits across interactive/scheduled mode, terminal occurrence | Not run | F02, F04 |
| G07 | Fast-completion handoff | Deterministic race barrier, occurrence terminal state, one associated run | Not run | F03 |
| G08 | Provider unavailable / malformed / timeout | Actual failure and consistent DB/API/UI/audit terminal state | Not run | F04, F05 |
| G09 | Persistence error during completion | Explicit degraded/error result, no false durable success | Not run | F04 |
| G10 | Scheduler per-job failure | Error surfaced; chosen safe behavior for later jobs | Not run | F05 |
| G11 | Approval expired while UI open | Correct countdown/count/error code; grant blocked | Backend expiry observed, UI incomplete | F07 |
| G12 | Reject pause and resume | No revision/enablement change after rejection | Not run | F22 |
| G13 | Same healthy DB restart before due | Future job/identity/skills retained and later execute as documented | Not run; fixture replaced instead | F03, F19, F22 |
| G14 | Same DB restart after completion | One-shot not accidentally replayed; occurrence state checked | Not run | F03, F22 |
| G15 | Crash during reservation/dispatch | Recoverable durable state and idempotency-aware reconciliation | Not run manually | F03, F04, F22 |
| G16 | Awaiting approval across restart | Explicit support/rejection/reconciliation; no silent lost authorization | Not run manually | F07, F14, F22 |
| G17 | Skill required vs optional invalidity | Typed exclusion; required blocks, optional warns per policy | Not run | F11 |
| G18 | Oversized/racing skill source | Bounded bytes/allocation and typed failure before injection | Not run | F12 |
| G19 | Meaningful capture/reuse | Tool-bearing source procedure reused on independent fixture | Not run | F13 |
| G20 | Wrong-token/workspace/offline browser | Verified health, no false empty or stale actionable rows | Wrong workspace observed, fixes pending | F08, F09 |
| G21 | Semantic approval review | Current exact target/diff/limits readable and fingerprint-bound | Generic preview observed | F10 |
| G22 | Shutdown with idle SSE and live model | Bounded exit, no post-stop scheduling, listeners gone | Not run | F14 |
| G23 | GPU-required restart/fallback | Controlled CPU-only/unknown rejection through managed path | GPU residency observed only | F20 |
| G24 | WSL memory attribution | Process/cache/GPU snapshots, host capacity and existing config | Not collected | F21 |
| G25 | Final platform/test inventory | Named-test count reconciliation and actual Linux desktop scope | Agent summary only; Linux desktop blocked | F23 |

For each run, retain: source SHA and dirty diff, build features/toolchain, fixture manifest, observed binary hash, timestamps from a trustworthy collection clock, exact request shape, status/body, identifiers, DB state assertions, final answer/effect, and cleanup results. Redact actual bearer tokens and credential values. Preserve unknowns rather than filling them with expected values.

## Corrections to earlier guidance and conclusions

| Earlier statement / approach | Corrected interpretation | Consequence |
|---|---|---|
| “Everything passed / full functional acceptance complete” | Positive administration and selected integrity controls passed; several essential agent/failure/restart assertions were missing | This report supersedes broad acceptance |
| A loaded model in ollama ps proves the Lumen run hit it | It proves loaded device residency, not caller/run attribution | Capture actual run result/provider evidence |
| 100% GPU means all memory/computation is GPU-only | Model residency is not absence of host RAM or CPU use | Separate device mode, RSS and VRAM |
| Completed GPU setup means every local model is enforced | External agent report/scripts were not supplied; arbitrary apps are outside a runtime's scope | Verify managed boundary and restart/fallback |
| Qwen marker refusal is irrelevant success | HTTP/model communication passed; requested response assertion failed | Record transport and task results separately |
| Equal timestamps imply instantaneous/fake execution | Source reuses one timestamp around awaited work | Fix clock, not speculate about mocks |
| next_due_at null proves successful execution | It means no next scheduled occurrence remains; occurrence/output require separate checks | Inspect run plus occurrence rows |
| One occurrence record proves exactly-once external side effects | Crash/lease/effect boundaries require stronger evidence and honest unknown state | Preserve non-idempotent reconciliation |
| Zero grants proves service cannot exceed permissions | Metadata was observed, no forbidden live action was attempted | Add a negative side-effect test |
| Empty captured workflow is a fully learned skill | It is a provenance draft, published and loaded; useful reuse unverified | Add a tool-bearing capture/reuse case |
| Successful digest rejection is enough | Silent missing required procedure can be operationally unsafe; read limit also enforced too late | Add exclusion telemetry and bounded read |
| Increasing scheduled time increases approval TTL | TTL remained five minutes independently | Fix expiry UX/test pacing, not weaken policy |
| 401 /health establishes health route | Middleware rejected before route handling; authenticated route was 404 | Document actual health/readiness |
| limit=250 is acceptable audit pagination | Source maximum is 200 | Correct helper and docs |
| jq .events[] failing means lost audit events | Error envelope lacks events; original raw response must be checked | Use explicit error handling |
| `/tmp` disappeared because a specific restart cleared it | Missing path observed; cause unknown | Keep persistent fixture manifest/snapshot |
| `rm -rf` silence is an error | Silence was normal; later recreation proved progress | Stop unnecessarily repeating delete commands |
| `^C` and shell closure guarantee all processes gone | Neither is a captured clean-exit/descendant/port proof | Use bounded stop + listener/PID check |
| WSL memory pressure is a Lumen leak | No attributable memory measurements were supplied | Diagnose before changing runtime/code/caps |
| Snap definitely caused GPU failure | Plausible packaging/WSL-library hypothesis, not isolated causal proof | Record remediation completion limits |
| ReplaceFileW is the final Windows repair | Later adversarial report replaced pathname handoff with handle-relative semantics | Use final head, preserve historical distinction |
| Fewer final tests prove weakening (or equivalence) | Count alone proves neither | Reconcile named tests and platform gates |
| Green Local runtime means authenticated readiness | It was driven by nonempty fields | Fix header/connection probe |
| Error plus zero rows means no pending actions | Failed read means unknown collection state | Distinct error/empty states |
| Compiling again is necessarily a Rust limitation | Target dir changed; other invalidation unmeasured | Diagnose cache reuse with exact environments |
| npm run dev caused pnpm-native mismatch | Running the script was not inherently wrong; missing platform package was observed | Isolate native installs/caches |

Historical instructions sometimes asked multiple chained operations despite the operator's preference for one or two steps. Future interactive guidance should identify the shell and active process ownership before each short block. The lengthy report is a record/specification, not a new interactive instruction sequence.

## Remediation sequence and branch boundaries

**No merges as part of this documentation/issue task. No edits to `base/ui`.** Keep the original checkout and Riley's in-progress branch untouched. Code fixes should use a separately named worktree/branch from the agreed M5 repair baseline. This report does not authorize arbitrary driver changes, broad local cleanup or service reconfiguration.

Suggested dependency-aware order (not a demand for one giant PR):

1. Establish accurate reproducible evidence and fixtures (F15/F17/F18/F19/F23) alongside focused deterministic regression tests.
2. Repair durability/limits: F03 handoff and F04 finalization together; F02 budgets; F05 scheduler errors; F06 clocks.
3. Complete existing model/tool integration and live assertions (F01) without taking on the full orchestration roadmap.
4. Repair lifecycle/inspection: F07 approvals, F14 shutdown, F11/F12 skill integrity/resource behavior.
5. Correct current UI state/previews (F08/F09/F10) while preserving the tested pending-state behavior; polish F24.
6. Run missing gate F22, then substantive capture/reuse F13. Keep F20 managed GPU verification and F21 environment diagnostics appropriately scoped.

These are suggested logical groups. Preserve small reviewable commits and a final independent review. A proposed branch name should be concise (for example `fix/m5-scheduler`, `fix/m5-lifecycle`, `fix/m5-controls`, `test/m5-acceptance`) and must not accidentally collide with existing work. Do not automatically create/merge stacked PRs without confirming the agreed baseline at implementation time.

**Non-goals:** no broad UI redesign, no new proprietary provider adapters, no full multi-agent task graph, no model-trust compartment implementation, no Windows sandbox weakening, no automatic audit-chain repair, and no user-wide memory/driver policy imposed by this issue-filing task. Existing issues #4/#5 remain the future architectural tracks.

### E02 — Pasted markdown.md

Agent final repair report. Its old safe-to-merge assessment is archived evidence, superseded by this QA review.


````markdown
PR [#6](https://github.com/black-candle-technologies/lumen/pull/6) is repaired, pushed, clean, and remains open/unmerged. Based on the completed review and verification, it is safe to merge into `feat/milestone-5-durable-automation`.

**Codex Security note:** Daybreak access was not granted for this account. That did not block the standard security scan; access is managed at [chatgpt.com/cyber](https://chatgpt.com/cyber).

## Issues found and repaired

| SeverityIssue and root causeRegression evidence and fix |                                                                                                                                          |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Medium                                                  | Workflow captures truncated provenance because the first 1,000 workspace events were fetched before filtering by run.                    | Added 1,001 older events plus an unrelated later run. Capture now uses an indexed `list_audit_records_for_run(workspace_id, run_id)` query and matches the exact ordered result.                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Medium                                                  | Model-egress audit events lacked `run_id`, so capture always excluded them.                                                              | Allowed/denied audit payload tests now require `run_id`; the scale regression explicitly captures a run-scoped `model_egress` event.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Medium                                                  | Cross-workspace capture checked workspace ownership only after inserting a draft.                                                        | Workspace is bound in the initial run lookup. Regression proves the request returns not-found and creates no draft.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Low                                                     | Windows replacement abandoned capability handles and re-resolved ambient paths through `ReplaceFileW`, creating a TOCTOU window.         | Replacement now renames the open temporary file relative to the open parent directory handle using documented [`FILE_RENAME_INFORMATION`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-hardware/drivers/ddi/ntifs/ns-ntifs-_file_rename_information) semantics. Existing unchanged/changed/mutated/new-target Windows tests pass. No live reparse-race PoC was attempted. |
| Low                                                     | Deferred SQLite read transactions failed closed with `SQLITE_BUSY` during claim/revision races, producing unstable durability semantics. | Both operations acquire `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` before reading. Deterministic two-connection tests cover duplicate claimers and claim versus service disable, newer revision, and job disable.                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Low                                                     | `saturating_add` obscured exhausted revision sequences at `i64::MAX`.                                                                    | Replaced with checked addition and added an overflow regression requiring `InvalidAutomationState`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

The original deferred SQLite implementation did not produce a stale successful claim in testing—it failed closed with a lock error. The repair makes that safety property explicit and deterministic.

## Changed-file review

All original 21 files were reviewed. The final diff contains 25 files because the capture repair added a migration, repository query, and migration-count updates.

- Test portability: [runtime/security_tests.rs](../../crates/lumen-cli/src/runtime/security_tests.rs), [commands.rs](../../crates/lumen-cli/tests/commands.rs), [extensions.rs](../../crates/lumen-cli/tests/extensions.rs), [support/mod.rs](../../crates/lumen-cli/tests/support/mod.rs), [database.rs](../../crates/lumen-db/tests/database.rs), [egress.rs](../../crates/lumen-db/tests/egress.rs), [db extensions.rs](../../crates/lumen-db/tests/extensions.rs), [extension_packages.rs](../../crates/lumen-integrations/tests/extension_packages.rs), [extension_process.rs](../../crates/lumen-integrations/tests/extension_process.rs), [local_executors.rs](../../crates/lumen-integrations/tests/local_executors.rs).
- Production portability: [config.rs](../../crates/lumen-cli/src/config.rs), [extension_runtime.rs](../../crates/lumen-cli/src/extension_runtime.rs), [lib.rs](../../crates/lumen-cli/src/lib.rs), [extension_package.rs](../../crates/lumen-integrations/src/extension_package.rs), [sandbox.rs](../../crates/lumen-integrations/src/sandbox.rs).
- Security/provenance: [runtime.rs](../../crates/lumen-cli/src/runtime.rs), [audit.rs](../../crates/lumen-db/src/audit.rs), [0006_audit_run_lookup.sql](../../crates/lumen-db/migrations/0006_audit_run_lookup.sql), [filesystem.rs](../../crates/lumen-integrations/src/filesystem.rs).
- Automation correctness: [automation.rs](../../crates/lumen-db/src/automation.rs), [automation tests](../../crates/lumen-db/tests/automation.rs).
- UI correctness: [automation/+page.svelte](../../apps/web/src/routes/automation/+page.svelte), [control-surface.spec.ts](../../apps/web/tests/control-surface.spec.ts).
- Dependency/metadata: [Cargo.toml](../../crates/lumen-integrations/Cargo.toml), [Cargo.lock](../../Cargo.lock).

No unrelated scope creep was found.

## Commits added

- `7e2049b` — `fix(automation): preserve capture provenance at scale`
- `a026593` — `fix(automation): serialize durable scheduler decisions`
- `fe915da` — `fix(filesystem): bind Windows replacement to handles`
- `12a725f` — `test(automation): cover captured model egress`
- `7c3080a` — `test(automation): cover exhausted revisions`

Final head: `7c3080ad1530fc7026b777fc211e2a68767e9e94`.

## Verification

Windows:

- Rust workspace: **259 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored**
- Vitest: **12 passed**
- Playwright: **14 passed** across desktop/mobile
- Svelte check: **0 errors, 0 warnings**
- Production web build: passed
- Formatting, clippy with warnings denied, and `git diff --check`: passed

WSL2 Ubuntu:

- `lumen-cli`: **76 passed**
- Automation concurrency/overflow: **11 passed**
- Integration library: **9 passed**, including all three Linux-only sandbox-profile tests
- Local executor/sandbox: **30 passed**
- Total executed: **126 passed, 0 failed, 0 ignored**
- Formatting and non-desktop workspace clippy: passed

Full Linux desktop verification was not claimed because the WSL image lacks the GTK/pkg-config prerequisites.

No assertion was weakened. No timeout was enlarged. The newly added 100 ms timeout is only a negative assertion that a claim remains blocked; barriers provide the synchronization.

Linux profile/report tests are gated to Linux because their production helpers and bubblewrap semantics exist only there. Unix symlink, signal, resource-limit, and process-group tests remain platform-gated and passed under WSL. No test was newly ignored.

## Review and residual risk

- Existing P2 thread: **resolved after the fix and passing verification**
- Remaining unresolved review threads: **0**
- GitHub Linux sandbox check: **passed**
- PR merge state: **CLEAN**
- Original checkout: untouched; its pre-existing untracked `lumen.toml` remains
- PR merged: **no**

Remaining known limitations are the intentionally unavailable/fail-closed Windows kernel sandbox, lack of a live hostile reparse-race PoC, and no full Linux desktop build. None contradicts the tested merge target’s documented guarantees.

The immutable Codex Security scan reported three findings—two medium and one low—all repaired by subsequent commits. The generated security report is retained with the raw evidence outside normal repository history; its former machine-local link is intentionally omitted. Token-usage metadata was unavailable from the completed scan, so no estimate is reported.
````

## Appendix B — model/HTTP/GPU transcript details

### B1. Direct local CLI final-marker test

Historical request:

```bash
ollama run qwen3:4b "Respond with exactly OLLAMA_OK"
```

The user pasted the following model-generated visible reasoning and final response. It is supplied test data, not this reviewer's private reasoning or proof of Lumen execution:


```text
Thinking...
Hmm, the user asked me to respond with exactly "OLLAMA_OK". Let me think about this.

First, I need to understand what they're looking for. Ollama is a tool for running large language models locally,
and they might be testing if I can follow specific instructions precisely. The key here is that they want the
response to be exactly "OLLAMA_OK" without any additional text or changes.

I should check if there's any context I'm missing. The user didn't provide any extra information, just that simple
request. My role is to respond exactly as instructed. Since they specified "exactly", I shouldn't add anything
else like explanations or greetings.

I recall that in some systems, especially in API responses or specific protocols, having a precise response string
is important. OLLAMA_OK is probably a status code or a response string they expect. My job here is to output that
string without modifications.

Wait, but I need to make sure I'm not introducing any unintended characters. The user said "exactly OLLAMA_OK", so
I should write it as is. Let me confirm the spelling: OLLAMA_OK (capital O, L, L, A, M, A, underscore, O, K). Yes,
that's correct.

I should avoid adding any newlines or extra spaces. The response should be just the string "OLLAMA_OK" followed by
a newline? Wait, the user said "exactly", so probably they want it as a clean string. But
in many systems, responses end with a newline. Hmm, the user might be expecting a clean string.

Looking back at the problem: "Respond with exactly OLLAMA_OK". So the answer should be the string "OLLAMA_OK" as
the response. Since this is a text response, I'll output that string.

I think the safest approach is to output "OLLAMA_OK" without any additional text. Let me make sure there's no
trick here. The user might be testing if I can follow instructions precisely. So I shouldn't add anything else.

Yes, so my response should be exactly "OLLAMA_OK". No extra spaces, no newlines, just the string. Wait, in some
contexts, responses have newlines, but the user said "exactly", so probably they want it as a clean string. Let me
check the standard practice. In API responses, sometimes it's just the string. Since they specified "exactly",
I'll go with that.

Alright, final decision: respond with "OLLAMA_OK".
...done thinking.

OLLAMA_OK
```

**Transcription note:** this is a transcription of user-pasted model output, not an independently collected terminal log. Line wrapping is not a behavioral assertion; the final marker is the test result. Model reasoning is untrusted output and its statements about the software are not adopted as facts.

### B5. Native dependency failure and recovery details

The key unresolved module and nested path were:

```text
Cannot find module '@rolldown/binding-linux-x64-gnu'
Cannot find module '../rolldown-binding.linux-x64-gnu.node'
MODULE_NOT_FOUND
.../node_modules/.pnpm/rolldown@1.0.3/node_modules/rolldown/dist/shared/binding-CXquf8ay.mjs
.../node_modules/.pnpm/vite@8.0.16_@types+node@25.9.3/node_modules/vite/dist/node/cli.js:706:27
Node.js v24.18.0
```

The error suggested npm optional-dependency trouble and deleting package-lock/node_modules. The actual repair instead respected the pnpm workspace: root/app node_modules were removed in this isolated worktree and `corepack pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` was run. It downloaded the Linux binding and the subsequent Vite launch succeeded. The command did not intentionally remove the lockfile. No final git diff was captured to independently prove all tracked files remained unchanged.

Slow-download warnings named `@testing-library/svelte-core` (4 KiB/s, 9 KiB), `@csstools/css-calc` (6 KiB/s, 12 KiB), `tinyexec` (3 KiB/s, 8 KiB), `mdn-data` (48 KiB/s, 100 KiB), and `@playwright/test` (4 KiB/s, 8 KiB), plus seven additional warnings not expanded in the pasted output. They did not prevent the completed install.

## Appendix D — delegated GPU configuration scope

The user required local inference to use the GPU and to make CPU-only fallback visible rather than silently continue. A separate local-access coding agent was asked to configure Windows/WSL/runtime behavior. Its completion was reported, but its full final report was not supplied. The following is the **requested verification checklist**, not additional evidence of work done.

| Phase | Requested work | Verification actually retained in this conversation |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Windows nvidia-smi; GPU, driver, VRAM, WDDM; wsl version/status/list | No Windows-side outputs attached |
| 2 | WSL nvidia-smi, /dev/dxg, /usr/lib/wsl/lib; host-driver passthrough | Later WSL nvidia-smi output only |
| 3 | Inspect snap, stop/remove safely, preserve model cache | Original snap service evidence; removal output not attached |
| 4 | Install official Linux Ollama; avoid duplicate daemon; ordinary service startup | Agent completion reported; version/path/service output not attached |
| 5 | Runtime CUDA detection/logs and qwen3:4b inference | GPU residency sample retained; CUDA detection logs absent |
| 6 | OpenAI-compatible API inference on GPU | Direct API JSON and subsequent GPU sample retained |
| 7 | Inventory installed Ollama/llama.cpp/vLLM/LM Studio/KoboldCpp/LocalAI/other backends, Docker/services/ports | Inventory absent |
| 8 | Configure discovered supported runtimes for NVIDIA without invented flags | Configuration files/change report absent |
| 9 | Machine GPU health-check script with nonzero CPU-only status | Script/output absent |
| 10 | Reusable verify-ollama-gpu model command | Script/output absent |
| 11 | Visible/fail-closed CPU fallback policy or wrapper | Negative test absent |
| 12 | Recommend provider device/offload metadata and require_gpu setting for Lumen without major source changes | Recommendation requested, implementation not evidenced |
| 13 | Record VRAM baseline/load/generation, utilization and processor split | One post-load sample only; no time series/baseline |
| 14 | Persist across shells, WSL shutdown/start, service restart, Windows restart and model changes | No repeatability matrix attached |
| 15 | Avoid breaking games, video encoding and unrelated GPU apps; safe driver handling | No damage reported; no comprehensive validation |
| 16 | Final direct/API inference, hardware/service/version/report inventory | Only selected post-fix inference/residency outputs retained |

Requested scripts were suggested at `C:\Users\<user>\bin\check-local-llm-gpu.ps1`, `~/bin/check-local-llm-gpu` and a `verify-ollama-gpu` helper. These are proposed paths, not confirmed existing files. The earlier agent prompt allowed partial CPU/RAM offload only as an explicitly necessary mode while preferring full model residency. Do not silently tighten that into a universal no-host-memory requirement or claim impossible control over unrelated executables.

The historical agent-selection advice named a specific GPT model and High reasoning. That name was not verified in this task and is not a current product recommendation. What mattered operationally was local Windows/WSL access and avoiding speculative driver changes.

Other suggested but unexecuted/unsupplied commands included `watch -n 0.5 nvidia-smi`, `systemctl status ollama`, checks of installed CUDA libraries, alternative Windows-host endpoint access, and runtime-specific torch.cuda.is_available checks. They remain guidance only. The preceding model response's claims about laws/policies are untrusted model-generated text, not diagnoses.

## Appendix E — reproducibility and evidence-retention contract

### Future execution record format

Each acceptance case should have a machine-readable companion roughly equivalent to:

```json
{
  "case_id": "G02",
  "source_sha": "<full-sha>",
  "worktree_dirty": false,
  "binary_sha256": "<actual-hash>",
  "environment": {"os": "<observed>", "shell": "<observed>", "toolchain": "<observed>"},
  "workspace_id": "<actual-fixture-uuid>",
  "started_at": "<collector-time>",
  "finished_at": "<collector-time>",
  "outcome": "pass|fail|expected_refusal|inconclusive|not_run",
  "commands": ["<redacted exact command>"],
  "http_status": 200,
  "run_id": "<actual-run>",
  "assertions": ["<explicit result checked>"],
  "evidence_paths": ["<retained-log>"],
  "limitations": ["<what was not established>"]
}
```

This is a proposed schema, not a claim that it existed during the session. Numeric examples above must be replaced with observed values.

### Guardrails for repetition

Use synthetic nonce files and disposable workspaces only. Preserve the healthy database through SQLite's backup API before intentional corruption. Keep per-fixture ownership metadata and reject missing/foreign roots. A negative test must positively assert the expected denial and absence of forbidden effect; silence is not proof. Do not hide errors with optional JSON iteration or classify a refused exact-marker response as task success.

Keep actual secrets out of logs, issue bodies and URLs. The token placeholder in this report is not a credential to use. Do not automatically install drivers, stop all WSL distributions, delete lockfiles, modify base/ui or grant all pending approvals. Stop only processes launched by the fixture, with bounded deadlines and independently checked listeners.

The complete uploaded logs below and screenshots constitute the recoverable historical evidence. The original deleted database, original tampered artifact and unprovided GPU/security-agent artifacts cannot be recovered from the report; a future test must collect fresh ones.

## GitHub issue coverage and verification ledger

The final GitHub state was reconciled before packaging. **22 canonical QA issues (#7–#28) are open**, and successful API mutation snapshots confirmed their labels. The matching QA issues were already present when the filing pass refreshed the repository, so they were reused and tagged rather than duplicated. A duplicate F01 issue (#29) was created during filing and then closed as a duplicate of #7; it is not an additional work item. No source files or branches were changed, and no PR was merged.

All observations are covered. Shared mappings are deliberate: terminal-state and scheduler diagnostics share #11; semantic previews and table presentation share #16; fixture launch and evidence backup share #21. The combined Rust/JavaScript environment observation is split into #24 and #25. The acceptance umbrella is #8.

Standard type labels were retained; `qa`, `priority:P1` or `priority:P2`, and appropriate `area:*` labels were applied and read back. `needs-validation` is used for proof gaps and risks awaiting reproduction. Priority is triage, not a security severity rating or proof of a runtime exploit. Any report priority adjusted to the canonical grouping is explicitly noted in its specification.

The filing pass corrected #23: only the invalid `limit=250` request is established by the retained transcript. Earlier issue wording mentioning observed 500/1,000 requests was not supported and was removed. The historical repaired 1,000-row workflow-capture limitation remains a separate fact.

### Final limits on conclusions

No test or evidence in this report authorizes a merge. Passing the repaired positive flows is meaningful, but essential live-agent and failure/recovery cases remain missing until explicitly completed. Fix reports must preserve the distinction between test observation, static mechanism and proposed behavior.
